• Sanae Kochiya adapts the fastest. Being originally from the Outside World, she happily slides into a casual high-waisted denim skirt and a green knit sweater, thrilled to look like a normal college student again.
• Marisa Kirisame grumbles loudly about losing her apron but compromises by stealing one of Sumireko’s oversized black hoodies, a pair of worn-out jeans, and a backward baseball cap—maintaining her tomboyish, street-style energy.
• Reimu Hakurei treats the entire process with absolute disdain. She fiercely protects her signature hair tubes and reluctantly settles for a plain red track jacket over a white t-shirt and sweatpants, looking incredibly lazy and mildly annoyed by the concept of "modern zippers."
• Kasen Ibaraki refuses to look sloppy. She meticulously wraps her smoky prosthetic arm in a long, elegant trench coat and slips into a conservative, calf-length pleated skirt, looking more like a strict Kyoto University professor than a regular student.
• The Boy stands by, tugging at the collar of a plain graphic tee and modern sneakers Sumireko threw at him, waiting for the girls to finish adjusting to the bizarre, restrictive textiles of the 21st century.
With that, Sumiriko boots up her computer and pulls up google maps but as she does a knkc comes to the door. Orb bot who is also there looks at him, and he looks to Orb Bot and states, still needs work, but he looks back to teh robots and states, now, go get me those emeralds.  The robots all head out one by one, follwoing their master's instructions.  Orb Bot then states, but sir, we still do not know where the creature CHaos is.  Yes, but we know how to find him. The craft is probably the size of an aircraft carrier, but whatever it is, he realizes that his rest break is probably over.  He calls for his cloud and when it arrives he hops right on it and flies off towards the downtown area of the city.

To him, these three teenagers represent a dangerous, localized rupture in the fabric of reality. Marisa immediately drops her anime pose, slides the Chaos Emerald securely into the pocket of her oversized stolen hoodie, and levels her mini-Hakkero directly at the floating wizard's face. Doctor Fate doesn't speak another word. He spreads his arms, and a massive, glowing golden Ankh burns into the air. Instantly, the gravity in the vault shatters. The floor becomes the ceiling, and the stone walls begin to compress outward in a perfect, mathematical grid designed to crush their spiritual focus. Sanae closes her eyes and triggers a localized miracle, altering the probability metrics of Fate's automated traps . The compressing walls suddenly jam on a statistical anomaly. Marisa fires a blinding, chaotic Master Spark into the reflective stone ceiling, turning the vault into a kaleidoscope of unguided rainbow lasers that overloads Nabu's sensory recalculations.  With Fate distracted by the probability glitches and laser reflections, Reimu triggers Fantasy Nature (Musou Tensei), floating completely out of reality . Totally intangible to Fate’s geometric binds, she walks straight through his cosmic shield and slaps a purification charm right onto his golden chest plate.  The spiritual explosion forces Doctor Fate to drop to one knee, the connection between Nabu and the mortal plane temporarily short-circuiting. Recognizing their absolute spiritual fortitude to handle the cosmic crisis, Fate yields the match, opening a massive golden portal that deposits the trio straight back onto the empty, grassy plot of land in Salem, Massachusetts. They stand on the muddy grass, completely out of breath, as the Tower vanishes back into the dimensional fabric behind them. Katana, still trapped in her Sailor Mars high heels, has to jump across spinning reel-platforms while golden coins rain down around her, seething because the machine keeps playing a high-pitched, upbeat pop jingle every time she successfully defends herself.

Absolutely — the key is to make Kasen the player/character controlling the pinball mechanism, rather than literally turning her into the ball.

:::writing{variant="document" id="58321" title="Refined Gameplay Concept"}

The Visual Theme: A massive, emerald-green and pastel-pink hybrid of a Pachinko machine and a classic Sonic pinball table. The artwork resembles a traditional, stylized Japanese water garden, complete with glowing lotus flowers, moving bridge bumpers, bamboo chutes, and neon koi swimming across digital screens behind the glass.
The Gameplay: In true Sonic Adventure fashion, Kasen is trapped inside the fantastical pinball environment but remains fully herself. Rather than becoming the ball, she controls and interacts with the machine from within the playfield, using her martial-arts skills and supernatural abilities to influence the trajectory of the pinball and navigate the board. She must manipulate bumpers, chutes, switches, and moving obstacles to guide the ball toward the target.
The Chaos: For someone as deeply dignified as Kasen, being trapped inside a wildly unpredictable pinball machine while dressed as Sailor Jupiter is already humiliating enough. She attempts to impose order on the chaos through precise martial-arts movements, redirecting the ball and manipulating the machine's mechanisms, but the Youkai physics constantly work against her. Rubber lotus bumpers send the ball ricocheting in unexpected directions, bamboo chutes suddenly change its course, and mischievous neon koi interfere with the playfield. To escape, Kasen must carefully control the sequence of bounces and guide the ball into a specific "Ideya Capture" center pocket, designed like a sacred shrine, while timing her movements around the shifting patterns of the neon koi.

3. Sumireko: The "Techno-Fortune" Roulette & Mahjong Grid

• The Visual Theme: A blindingly bright, futuristic blue and purple digital tabletop game that blends a giant roulette wheel with a matching Mahjong slot matrix. The artwork features holographic lucky cats (Maneki-neko), neon jade tokens, and intricate geometric patterns that shift color depending on the odds.
• The Gameplay: Sumireko (Sailor Mercury) is placed at the center of a massive, spinning roulette wheel where the numbers are represented by glowing Mahjong tiles. She has to physically run along the spinning outer ring—much like the BINGO Highway or speed tunnels in Sonic lore—to collect specific floating data cards to build a winning hand.
• The Chaos: While the physical exertion is annoying, this game plays entirely into Sumireko's analytical mind. Using her psychic powers, she can actually "see" the internal RNG (Random Number Generator) code of the Youkai machine. As the wheel spins at dizzying speeds, she uses her telekinesis to subtly alter the weight of the ball or shift the digital tiles, quietly turning the Youkai's rigged trap into an absolute mathematical sweep.

Chaos 6 is a complete biological departure from the liquid beast's previous humanoid forms. Fueled by the negative energy of six Chaos Emeralds, it has mutated into a colossal, grotesque, non-humanoid leviathan standing over three times the height of Big the Cat.
• The Silhouette: Its massive, transparent-blue water body vaguely resembles a bloated scorpion, spider, and frog hybrid. It has six distinct legs: two pairs of digit-less front legs and a massive pair of grasshopper-like hind legs that bend backward.
• The Head and Tail: It features a wide, bulldog-like head splitting into a giant, gaping mouth lined with razor-sharp teeth. It possesses multiple emerald-green eyes: two large eyes aligned vertically on the front of its face, flanked by three smaller eyes aligned horizontally on each side. A long, zigzagged scorpion tail loops over its back, ending in a blunt tip.
• The Core and Extended Skeleton: Because it is translucent, you can see a terrifying internal bone structure running through its fluid body. Visible gray bones run down its hind legs, connected at the rear by a pelvis-like frame. Encircling its brain inside its belly are four prominent, upright rib-like bones. The six Chaos Emeralds are explicitly visible embedded directly into its skeletal structure—one in each hind leg bone, and four sitting atop the central ribs surrounding its exposed core.

Functional Description & Powers

Because of the sheer density of cosmic power flowing through its transparent body, Chaos 6 cannot be harmed by regular physical attacks. Its brain is situated too deeply within its bone-shielded core to suffer impact damage, rendering standard superhero strikes completely useless. Functionally, it acts as a walking tidal wave with three terrifying combat mechanics:
• Vacuum Breath (Inhale Attack): Due to its massive lung capacity and fluid body structure, it can open its giant mouth to create a localized, hurricane-force vortex. It violently sucks in surrounding air to draw enemies directly inside its body to consume them or trap them within its watery mass.
• Advanced Hydrokinetic Shapeshifting: It no longer just morphs its limbs into simple fists. It can violently reshape its entire body into a towering pillar of water, sprout localized spikes from its skin, or extend long tentacles to strike targets from across the deck.
• Energy Waves and Shockwaves: By leaping into the air and slamming its immense mass down onto the deck plates, it converts the physical weight of its 6-emerald power into devastating, kinetic shockwaves that ripple outward, shattering solid stone and mechanical bulkheads alike

Key Battle Mechanics

• Phase 1 (Lasers & Buzzsaws): Eggman fires standard lasers that you can easily outrun, followed by an exploding mega-laser. He will occasionally launch spinning buzzsaws that you must jump on to ride across gaps.
• Phase 2 (Tail Chain): The Egg Viper will retreat into the background and unleash its tail segments. You must accurately chain Homing Attacks up the tail pieces to reach the cockpit and strike the weak point.
• Phase 3 (Platform Destruction): When the mech's health drops low, it begins actively smashing and destroying the platforms, putting a strict time limit on the encounter before you plummet to your death.
• Kamikaze Move: As soon as the final boss's health bar is fully depleted, it will execute one last desperate ramming attack. To avoid dying at the last second, immediately run to the very edge of the remaining platform and stay still so the crashing ship misses you

The base launched a number of smaller mech units. 
To make this quick here is what they have:

1. Hunter Series (Humanoid Foot Soldiers)

• Gun Hunter: The most basic model of the series and the baseline design for other variants. It features a blue, white, red, gray, and yellow color scheme with a single red face eye, tracking targets and using projectile gun or missile attacks.
• Laser Hunter: A variation equipped with continuous or hazardous laser weaponry.
• Shield Hunter: Features a large, defensive red shield that blocks incoming attacks; it becomes vulnerable only when attacking or exposed from behind.

2. Beetle Series (Aerial Drones)

• Gun Beetle / Standard Beetles: Small, flying reconnaissance and combat drones coming in multiple sub-variants (such as Bomb, Gold, Gun, and Spark) that use laser fire and electric fields.

3. Hawk Series (Aerial Drones)

• Gun Hawk: A larger aerial unit resembling an upgraded Gun Beetle, featuring twin engines, underside spike weapons, white eyes, and a vertical projectile gun.

4. Hornet Series (Aerial Drones)

• Fast-flying aerial units (including variants like the Phoenix) designed for scout and air-to-ground engagement.

5. Rhino Series (Ground / Wheel-Based Units)

• Rhino Robots: Wheel-based combat units built for fast rolling charges and ground pressure across highway and urban stages.

6. Heavy Combat Mechs & Walkers

• Big Foot: A heavy, bipedal combat mecha often piloted or encountered as a mini-boss, firing missiles and tracking the player with heavy stomping attacks.
• Mobile Units / Special Assets: Includes larger vehicles and specialized units like the Blue Falcon or Heavy Dog, as well as prototype energy weapons like the Diablon.
